########## IN_RANGE
LAD  ·  Comparison / selection

== What it does ==
A contact box: the power goes through when MIN <= VAL <= MAX. The data type is chosen in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  MIN | number | the lower limit
  VAL | number | the value
  MAX | number | the upper limit

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put it on the rung like a contact; type MIN, VAL and MAX.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] inrange@Int 20|"Temp"|30;coil "Temp_Ok"

== Values ==
  MIN | VAL | MAX | power
  20 | 25 | 30 | on
  20 | 35 | 30 | off
The same in SCL:
    "Temp_Ok" := ("Temp" >= 20) AND ("Temp" <= 30);

########## OUT_RANGE
LAD  ·  Comparison / selection

== What it does ==
A contact box: the power goes through when VAL < MIN or VAL > MAX. The data type is chosen in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  MIN | number | the lower limit
  VAL | number | the value
  MAX | number | the upper limit

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put it on the rung like a contact; type MIN, VAL and MAX.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] outrange@Int 20|"Temp"|30;coil "Temp_Alarm"

== Values ==
  MIN | VAL | MAX | power
  20 | 25 | 30 | off
  20 | 35 | 30 | on
The same in SCL:
    "Temp_Alarm" := ("Temp" < 20) OR ("Temp" > 30);

########## SEL
LAD  ·  Comparison / selection

== What it does ==
Selects one of two values: G = FALSE gives IN0, G = TRUE gives IN1.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  G | Bool | the selector
  IN0 | any | the value for G = FALSE
  IN1 | any | the value for G = TRUE
  OUT | any | IN0 or IN1

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";sel@Real "Night"|21.0|17.0|"Setpoint";-
The same in SCL:
    "Setpoint" := SEL(G := "Night", IN0 := 21.0, IN1 := 17.0);

== Values ==
  G | IN0 | IN1 | OUT
  FALSE | 21.0 | 17.0 | 21.0
  TRUE | 21.0 | 17.0 | 17.0

########## MUX
LAD  ·  Comparison / selection

== What it does ==
Selects the input number K: K = 0 gives IN0, K = 1 IN1 ... A K that has no input gives INELSE (0 when there is none) and ENO = FALSE.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  K | Int | the number of the input
  IN0 .. IN31 | any | the values
  INELSE | any | the value for a K outside (optional)
  OUT | any | the selected value

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";mux@Int "Gear"|0|300|800|"Speed";-
The same in SCL:
    "Speed" := MUX(K := "Gear", IN0 := 0, IN1 := 300, IN2 := 800, INELSE := 0);

== Values ==
  K | IN0 | IN1 | IN2 | OUT
  0 | 10 | 20 | 30 | 10
  1 | 10 | 20 | 30 | 20
  2 | 10 | 20 | 30 | 30
  3 | 10 | 20 | 30 | INELSE (else 0)

== Good to know ==
- The LAD box has IN0 .. IN2; SCL takes up to IN31.

########## LIMIT
LAD  ·  Comparison / selection

== What it does ==
Limits IN to the range MN .. MX: below MN the result is MN, above MX it is MX, in between IN itself.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  MN | number | the lower limit
  IN | number | the value
  MX | number | the upper limit
  OUT | number | IN, limited

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";limit@Real 0.0|"PID_Out"|100.0|"Valve_Pos";-
The same in SCL:
    "Valve_Pos" := LIMIT(MN := 0.0, IN := "PID_Out", MX := 100.0);

== Values ==
  MN | IN | MX | OUT
  0 | -5 | 100 | 0
  0 | 42 | 100 | 42
  0 | 130 | 100 | 100

== Good to know ==
- MN must be <= MX.

########## MIN
LAD  ·  Comparison / selection

== What it does ==
Returns the smallest of its inputs (2 .. 32 in SCL, up to 4 in the LAD box). All inputs have the same data type (Int, DInt, Real, Time ..).

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN1 | number | the first value
  IN2 | number | the second value
  IN3 .. | number | more values (optional)
  OUT | number | the smallest

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";min@Int "Speed_HMI"|"Speed_Max"|||"Speed_Set";-
The same in SCL:
    "Speed_Set" := MIN(IN1 := "Speed_HMI", IN2 := "Speed_Max");

== Values ==
  IN1 | IN2 | IN3 | OUT
  5 | 9 |  | 5
  -3 | 2 | 7 | -3
  2.5 | 2.4 |  | 2.4

== Good to know ==
- Mixed types need a conversion first (INT_TO_REAL ..).

########## MAX
LAD  ·  Comparison / selection

== What it does ==
Returns the largest of its inputs (2 .. 32 in SCL, up to 4 in the LAD box).

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN1 | number | the first value
  IN2 | number | the second value
  IN3 .. | number | more values
  OUT | number | the largest

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";max@Int "Level_Peak"|"Level"|||"Level_Peak";-
The same in SCL:
    "Level_Peak" := MAX(IN1 := "Level_Peak", IN2 := "Level");

== Values ==
  IN1 | IN2 | IN3 | OUT
  5 | 9 |  | 9
  -3 | 2 | 7 | 7
  2.5 | 2.4 |  | 2.5

########## ADD
LAD  ·  Math functions

== What it does ==
Adds: OUT := IN1 + IN2. The data type is chosen in the box (Int, DInt, Real ..); all pins have it.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN1 | number | the first value
  IN2 | number | the second value
  OUT | number | the result

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";add@Int "Count_A"|"Count_B"|"Total";-
The same in SCL:
    "Total" := "Count_A" + "Count_B";

== Values ==
  IN1 | IN2 | OUT
  5 | 3 | 8

== Good to know ==
- An Int result that does not fit wraps around (32767 + 1 = -32768): use DInt or Real for large values.
- The box runs in every cycle while EN has power: for "once" use a P contact in front of it.

########## DIV
LAD  ·  Math functions

== What it does ==

== Syntax ==

  7 | 2 | 3
  8 | 0 | 0 (ENO = 0)

== Good to know ==

- The box runs in every cycle while EN has power: for "once" use a P contact in front of it.

########## NEG
LAD  ·  Math functions

== What it does ==
Changes the sign: OUT := -IN.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN | number | the value
  OUT | number | -IN

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";negate@Int "Value"|"Minus";-
The same in SCL:
    "Minus" := -"Value";

== Values ==
  IN | OUT
  5 | -5
  -3 | 3

########## INC
LAD  ·  Math functions

== What it does ==
Adds 1 to the operand in every cycle while the power is on. For once per event put a P contact in front.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN_OUT | integer | the operand that is changed

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] p "Sensor"|"Edge_M";inc@Int "Count";-
The same in SCL:
    "Count" := "Count" + 1;

########## EXPT
LAD  ·  Math functions

== What it does ==
The power: OUT := IN1 ** IN2 (a Real). The data type is chosen in the box (Int, DInt, Real ..); all pins have it.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN1 | number | the first value
  IN2 | number | the second value
  OUT | number | the result

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";expt@Int "Count_A"|"Count_B"|"Total";-
The same in SCL:
    "Total" := "Count_A" ** "Count_B";

== Values ==
  IN1 | IN2 | OUT
  2.0 | 3.0 | 8.0

== Good to know ==
- An Int result that does not fit wraps around (32767 + 1 = -32768): use DInt or Real for large values.
- The box runs in every cycle while EN has power: for "once" use a P contact in front of it.

########## ABS
LAD  ·  Math functions

== What it does ==
The absolute value: -5 gives 5. Works for integers and Real.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN | Real / LReal | the value (also an integer)
  OUT | Real / LReal | ABS(IN)

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.

5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";abs@Real "Value"|"Result";-
The same in SCL:
    "Result" := ABS("Value");

== Values ==
  IN | ABS(IN)
  0.0 | 0.0
  0.5 | 0.5
  2.0 | 2.0
  -1.0 | 1.0
  (computed with the same functions as the CPU)

== Good to know ==
- The result of an Real input is a Real, of an LReal an LReal.
- An invalid input gives NaN and ENO = FALSE; check it with the ENO of the box or before the call.

########## MOVE

5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Start";move@Int 1500|"Speed_Set";-
The same in SCL:
    "Speed_Set" := 1500;

== Good to know ==
- Without power the target keeps its value.

########## S_MOVE
LAD  ·  Move / conversion

== What it does ==
Copies a string into another one. The target keeps its own maximum length: a longer string is cut.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN | String | the source
  OUT | String | the target

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Load";smove #Name|"Recipe_Name";-
The same in SCL:
    "Recipe_Name" := #Name;

########## CONV
LAD  ·  Move / conversion

== What it does ==
Converts IN from one data type into another (the types of IN and OUT are chosen in the box): Int to Real, Real to DInt (rounded), Word to Int ..

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN | the IN type | the value
  OUT | the OUT type | the converted value

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";conv@Int>Real "Speed_I"|"Speed_R";-
The same in SCL:
    "Speed_R" := INT_TO_REAL("Speed_I");

== Values ==
  IN (Int) | OUT (Real)
  5 | 5.0
  -300 | -300.0

== Good to know ==
- A value the target cannot hold: ENO = 0.

########## ROUND
LAD  ·  Move / conversion

== What it does ==
Converts a Real into an integer (DInt) to the nearest whole number; a half goes to the even one (2.5 gives 2, 3.5 gives 4).

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN | Real / LReal | the value
  OUT | DInt (LAD: the type in the box) | the whole number

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";round@Real>DInt "Litres"|"Count";-
The same in SCL:
    "Count" := ROUND("Litres" / 2.5);

== Values ==
  IN | ROUND
  2.4 | 2
  2.5 | 2
  3.5 | 4
  2.6 | 3
  -2.5 | -2
  -2.6 | -3

== Good to know ==
- A Real that is too large for the target wraps around: check the range first.
- STL: RND, RND+, RND-, TRUNC work on ACCU 1.

########## TRUNC
LAD  ·  Move / conversion

== What it does ==
Converts a Real into an integer (DInt) by cutting the decimals off: 2.9 gives 2, -2.9 gives -2.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";trunc@Real>DInt "Litres"|"Count";-
The same in SCL:

== Values ==

  2.5 | 2
  3.5 | 3
  2.6 | 2
  -2.5 | -2
  -2.6 | -2

########## NORM_X
LAD  ·  Move / conversion

== What it does ==
Normalizes VALUE from the range MIN .. MAX to 0.0 .. 1.0: OUT := (VALUE - MIN) / (MAX - MIN). With SCALE_X it turns a raw analog value (0 .. 27648) into a physical one.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  MIN | Int / Real | the start of the range
  VALUE | Int / Real | the value
  MAX | Int / Real | the end of the range
  OUT | Real | 0.0 .. 1.0

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";normx@Int 0|"AI_Level"|27648|#n;- / no "Enable";scalex@Real 0.0|#n|5.0|"Level_m";-
The same in SCL:
    #n := NORM_X(MIN := 0, VALUE := "AI_Level", MAX := 27648);
    "Level_m" := SCALE_X(MIN := 0.0, VALUE := #n, MAX := 5.0);

== Values ==
  MIN | VALUE | MAX | OUT
  0 | 0 | 27648 | 0.0
  0 | 13824 | 27648 | 0.5
  0 | 27648 | 27648 | 1.0
  0 | 30000 | 27648 | 1.085
  (a value outside MIN .. MAX gives less than 0.0 or more than 1.0)

########## SCALE_X
LAD  ·  Move / conversion

== What it does ==
Scales VALUE from 0.0 .. 1.0 to the range MIN .. MAX: OUT := VALUE * (MAX - MIN) + MIN. After NORM_X it gives the physical value; before an analog output it turns 0 .. 1 into 0 .. 27648.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  MIN | Int / Real | the start of the range
  VALUE | Real | 0.0 .. 1.0
  MAX | Int / Real | the end of the range
  OUT | Int / Real | the scaled value

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";scalex@Int 0|#Frac|27648|"AQ_Valve";-
The same in SCL:
    "AQ_Valve" := SCALE_X(MIN := 0, VALUE := "Valve_Pos" / 100.0, MAX := 27648);

== Values ==
  MIN | VALUE | MAX | OUT
  0.0 | 0.0 | 5.0 | 0.0
  0.0 | 0.5 | 5.0 | 2.5
  4.0 | 0.25 | 20.0 | 8.0

########## SHL
LAD  ·  Shift / rotate / word logic

== What it does ==
Shifts the bits of IN N places to the left; 0s come in from the right, the bits that leave on the left are lost. For an unsigned number: IN * 2^N.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN | Byte / Word / DWord (integers too) | the bit pattern
  N | UInt | how many places
  OUT | as IN | the result

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Step";shl@Word "Pattern"|3|"Mask";-
The same in SCL:
    "Mask" := SHL(IN := "Pattern", N := 3);

== The bits ==
  [bits] IN = 16#00B3=0000000010110011 / SHL(IN, N := 3) = 16#0598=0000010110011ooo
  (the bits that came in are red)

== Good to know ==
- STL itself: SLW / SRW / SLD / SRD shift ACCU 1, RLD / RRD rotate it.
- A running light: ROL of a Word once per second.

########## SHR
LAD  ·  Shift / rotate / word logic

== What it does ==
Shifts the bits of IN N places to the right; 0s come in from the left. For an unsigned number: IN / 2^N.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN | Byte / Word / DWord (integers too) | the bit pattern
  N | UInt | how many places
  OUT | as IN | the result

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.

5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Step";shr@Word "Pattern"|3|"Mask";-
The same in SCL:

== The bits ==
  [bits] IN = 16#00B3=0000000010110011 / SHR(IN, N := 3) = 16#0016=ooo0000000010110
  (the bits that came in are red)

== Good to know ==
- STL itself: SLW / SRW / SLD / SRD shift ACCU 1, RLD / RRD rotate it.
- A running light: ROL of a Word once per second.

########## ROL
LAD  ·  Shift / rotate / word logic

Rotates the bits of IN N places to the left: the bits that leave on the left come in again on the right.

    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Step";rol@Word "Pattern"|3|"Mask";-

== Good to know ==

########## AND (word)
LAD  ·  Shift / rotate / word logic

== What it does ==
Combines IN1 and IN2 bit by bit (AND). The data type (Byte, Word, DWord) is chosen in the box.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN1 | Word | the bits
  IN2 | Word | the mask
  OUT | Word | the result

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";andw@Word "Status"|16#00FF|#Out;-
The same in SCL:
    #Out := "Status" AND 16#00FF;

== The bits ==
  [bits] IN1 = 16#5AF3=0101101011110011 / IN2 = 16#00FF=0000000011111111 / OUT = 16#00F3=00000000iiiiooii
  (the bits of the result are red)

########## INV
LAD  ·  Shift / rotate / word logic

== What it does ==
Inverts every bit of IN. The data type (Byte, Word, DWord) is chosen in the box.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN | Word | the bits
  OUT | Word | NOT IN

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";invw@Word "Status"|#Out;-
The same in SCL:
    #Out := NOT "Status";

== The bits ==
  [bits] IN = 16#5AF3=0101101011110011 / OUT = 16#A50C=ioiooioiooooiioo
  (the bits of the result are red)

########## CONCAT
LAD  ·  String

== What it does ==
Joins IN2 to the end of IN1. The result is cut at the maximum length of the target.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN1 | String | the first part
  IN2 | String | the second part
  OUT | String | IN1 followed by IN2

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Show";concat 'Station '|"Station_Name"|#Msg;-
The same in SCL:
    #Msg := CONCAT(IN1 := 'Station ', IN2 := "Station_Name");

== Values ==
  IN1 | IN2 | OUT
  'AB' | 'CD' | 'ABCD'
  'Tank ' | '3' | 'Tank 3'

== Good to know ==
- More parts: CONCAT inside CONCAT.

########## LEN
LAD  ·  String

== What it does ==
The current length of a string (the number of characters in it, not its maximum).

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN | String | the string
  OUT | Int | its length

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Read";len "Barcode"|"Chars";-
The same in SCL:
    "Chars" := LEN("Barcode");

== Values ==
  IN | OUT
  'ABC' | 3
  '' | 0
  'Line 1' | 6

########## LEFT
LAD  ·  String

== What it does ==
The first L characters of IN.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN | String | the string
  L | Int | how many characters
  OUT | String | the first L

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.

5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Read";left "Barcode"|3|#Code;-
The same in SCL:
    #Code := LEFT(IN := "Barcode", L := 3);

== Values ==
  IN | L | OUT
  'ABCDEF' | 3 | 'ABC'
  'AB' | 5 | 'AB'

########## MID
LAD  ·  String

== What it does ==
L characters of IN from the position P (the first character is position 1).

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN | String | the string
  L | Int | how many characters
  P | Int | from which position (1 = the first)
  OUT | String | the part

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Read";mid "Barcode"|2|4|#Lot;-
The same in SCL:
    #Lot := MID(IN := "Barcode", L := 2, P := 4);

== Values ==
  IN | L | P | OUT
  'ABCDEF' | 2 | 3 | 'CD'
  'ABCDEF' | 3 | 5 | 'EF'

########## FIND
LAD  ·  String

== What it does ==
The position of the string IN2 in IN1 (1 = at the start); 0 when it is not there.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN1 | String | where to look
  IN2 | String | what to find
  OUT | Int | its position, 0: not found

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Read";find "Barcode"|'-'|#Pos;-
The same in SCL:
    #Pos := FIND(IN1 := "Barcode", IN2 := '-');

== Values ==
  IN1 | IN2 | OUT
  'AB-CD' | '-' | 3
  'ABCD' | 'X' | 0

########## S_MOVE
LAD  ·  String

== What it does ==
Copies a string into another one. The target keeps its own maximum length: a longer string is cut.

== Syntax ==
    a box on the rung: EN is the rung on its left, ENO the power out (ENO = EN; FALSE when the result is invalid).
    The inputs on its left, the output on its right; the data type in the box.

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  IN | String | the source
  OUT | String | the target

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put the condition contact (or nothing: the box runs always), then the box on the next cell.
4. Click the box: type the inputs and the output in the properties panel on the right; choose the data type there.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Load";smove #Name|"Recipe_Name";-
The same in SCL:
    "Recipe_Name" := #Name;

########## CALL
LAD  ·  Program control

== What it does ==
Calls an FC or an FB. The box shows the parameters of the block as pins: Inputs and InOuts on the left, Outputs and the return value on the right. EN = 0 skips the call.

== Syntax ==
    a box: the block (FC) or the instance DB of the FB above it; the pins are its parameters

== How to use it ==
1. Click the cell of the LAD network where the element goes (the cell gets a gold frame). A contact goes on the rung from the left, a coil or a box after the contacts that switch it.
2. Double click the element in this list, or drag it by its grip (the dots) onto a cell (a wire or an empty cell). Several cells: Shift+click; a new row: + Row below the panel.
3. Put a CALL on the rung, then type the FC name or the instance DB above it (or double click an FC / an instance DB in the category Project of this list: it comes with its pins).
4. Fill in the pins in the properties panel; an FC needs every Input / Output / InOut.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) draws the power flow in red and the values at the pins.
  [LAD] no "Enable";call "Motor1_DB";-;coil "Motor1_Run"
  (an FB with its instance DB)
The same in SCL:
    "Motor1_DB"(Start := "Start_PB", Stop := "Stop_PB", Run => "Motor1");

########## Del / Ctrl+X / Ctrl+C / Ctrl+V
LAD  ·  Editor keys

== What it does ==
Delete, cut, copy, paste (inserts) the selected cells.

== How to use it ==
1. Select cells: click one, Shift+click the last one (or a rubber band with the mouse).
2. Del deletes them, Ctrl+X cuts, Ctrl+C copies; click the target cell and Ctrl+V inserts them there.

########## Shift+click, Shift+arrows
LAD  ·  Editor keys

== What it does ==
A selection of cells; Ctrl+A the network.

== How to use it ==
1. Click the first cell, then Shift+click the last one, or Shift+arrows from the selected cell.
2. Ctrl+A selects the whole network.

########## drag an element
LAD  ·  Editor keys

== What it does ==
Moves the cells (into another network too, onto wires).

== How to use it ==
1. Press the mouse on an element (or on the selected cells) and drag: a ghost follows the mouse.
2. The red frame shows where they land; grey: no room there. Release to drop them (also into another network).

########## Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y
LAD  ·  Editor keys

== What it does ==
Undo / redo.

== How to use it ==
1. Ctrl+Z undoes the last change (200 steps), Ctrl+Y does it again (also the Undo / Redo buttons).

########## A
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==
AND: the RLO := RLO AND the operand (the first check loads it). STL works with the RLO (the result of logic operation): the first check of a sequence loads it, the following ones combine it, = / S / R write it.

== Syntax ==
    A     <bit operand>      // %I0.0, "Start", #In, "DB".Flag, T 1, C 1

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write one check per line, then = (or S / R) with the output.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== The RLO line by line ==
  Line | "Start" | "Door" | RLO after it
  A     "Start" | 1 | 1 | 1 (loaded)
  A    "Door" | 1 | 1 | 1
  =     "Motor" |  |  | "Motor" := RLO

== The same in LAD ==
  [LAD] no "Start";no "Door";coil "Motor"

== Example ==
    A     "Start"
    A    "Door_Closed"
    =     "Motor"

########## AN
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==
AND NOT. STL works with the RLO (the result of logic operation): the first check of a sequence loads it, the following ones combine it, = / S / R write it.

== Syntax ==
    AN     <bit operand>      // %I0.0, "Start", #In, "DB".Flag, T 1, C 1

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write one check per line, then = (or S / R) with the output.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== The RLO line by line ==
  Line | "Start" | "Door" | RLO after it
  A     "Start" | 1 | 1 | 1 (loaded)
  AN    "Door" | 1 | 1 | 0
  =     "Motor" |  |  | "Motor" := RLO

== The same in LAD ==
  [LAD] no "Start";nc "Door";coil "Motor"

== Example ==
    A     "Start"
    AN    "Door_Closed"
    =     "Motor"

########## O
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==
OR. STL works with the RLO (the result of logic operation): the first check of a sequence loads it, the following ones combine it, = / S / R write it.

== Syntax ==
    O     <bit operand>      // %I0.0, "Start", #In, "DB".Flag, T 1, C 1

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write one check per line, then = (or S / R) with the output.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== The RLO line by line ==
  Line | "Start" | "Jog" | RLO after it
  A     "Start" | 0 | 1 | 0
  O    "Jog" | 0 | 1 | 1
  =     "Motor" |  |  | "Motor" := RLO

== The same in LAD ==
  [LAD] no "Start"!;-;coil "Motor" / no "Jog"

== Example ==
    A     "Start"
    O    "Door_Closed"
    =     "Motor"

########## ON
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==
OR NOT. STL works with the RLO (the result of logic operation): the first check of a sequence loads it, the following ones combine it, = / S / R write it.

== Syntax ==
    ON     <bit operand>      // %I0.0, "Start", #In, "DB".Flag, T 1, C 1

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.

4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== The RLO line by line ==
  Line | "Start" | "Jog" | RLO after it
  A     "Start" | 0 | 1 | 0
  ON    "Jog" | 0 | 1 | 0
  =     "Motor" |  |  | "Motor" := RLO

== The same in LAD ==
  [LAD] no "Start"!;-;coil "Motor" / nc "Jog"

== Example ==
    A     "Start"
    ON    "Door_Closed"
    =     "Motor"

########## X
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==
Exclusive OR. STL works with the RLO (the result of logic operation): the first check of a sequence loads it, the following ones combine it, = / S / R write it.

== The same in LAD ==

    =     "Motor"

########## XN
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==

  Line | "A" | "B" | RLO after it

########## A(
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==
AND of a bracket: A( .. ). STL works with the RLO (the result of logic operation): the first check of a sequence loads it, the following ones combine it, = / S / R write it.

== Syntax ==
    A(
      O     "A"
      O     "B"
    )
    A     "C"
    =     "Q"

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Open the bracket, write the checks inside, close it with ); the bracket result is combined with the RLO before it.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== The same in LAD ==
  [LAD] no "A"!;no "C";coil "Q" / no "B"
  ("Q" := ("A" OR "B") AND "C")

== Example ==
    A(
    O     "A"
    O     "B"
    )
    A     "C"
    =     "Q"

########## O(
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==
OR of a bracket. STL works with the RLO (the result of logic operation): the first check of a sequence loads it, the following ones combine it, = / S / R write it.

== Syntax ==

      O     "A"
      O     "B"
    )
    A     "C"
    =     "Q"

  ("Q" := ("A" OR "B") AND "C")

== Example ==
    A(
    O     "A"
    O     "B"
    )
    A     "C"
    =     "Q"

########## )
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==
The end of a bracket. STL works with the RLO (the result of logic operation): the first check of a sequence loads it, the following ones combine it, = / S / R write it.

== Syntax ==

      O     "A"

########## O
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==
OR (an empty O): AND before OR. STL works with the RLO (the result of logic operation): the first check of a sequence loads it, the following ones combine it, = / S / R write it.

== Syntax ==
    A     "A"
    A     "B"
    O
    A     "C"
    A     "D"
    =     "Q"

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. An O without an operand between two AND groups: AND is done before OR.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.
  [LAD] no "A";no "B"!;coil "Q" / no "C";no "D"
  ("Q" := ("A" AND "B") OR ("C" AND "D"))

== Example ==
    A     "A"
    A     "B"
    O
    A     "C"
    A     "D"
    =     "Q"

########## =
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==
Assignment: the operand := RLO. STL works with the RLO (the result of logic operation): the first check of a sequence loads it, the following ones combine it, = / S / R write it.

== Syntax ==
    =     <bit operand>

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write it after the checks: the operand gets the RLO.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Example ==
    A     "Start"
    S     "Motor"
    A     "Stop"
    R     "Motor"

########## S
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==
Set the operand while RLO = 1. STL works with the RLO (the result of logic operation): the first check of a sequence loads it, the following ones combine it, = / S / R write it.

== Syntax ==
    S     <bit operand>

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write it after the checks: while the RLO is 1 the operand is set (TRUE); it keeps the value when the RLO is 0.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Timing diagram ==
  [timing] Start=0110000001100000 / Stop=0000011000000110 / Motor=0111100001111000
  (A "Start" S "Motor" / A "Stop" R "Motor")

== Example ==
    A     "Start"
    S     "Motor"
    A     "Stop"
    R     "Motor"

########## R
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==
Reset the operand (also a timer / counter). STL works with the RLO (the result of logic operation): the first check of a sequence loads it, the following ones combine it, = / S / R write it.

== Syntax ==
    R     <bit operand>

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write it after the checks: while the RLO is 1 the operand is reset (FALSE); it keeps the value when the RLO is 0.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Timing diagram ==
  [timing] Start=0110000001100000 / Stop=0000011000000110 / Motor=0111100001111000
  (A "Start" S "Motor" / A "Stop" R "Motor")

== Example ==
    A     "Start"
    S     "Motor"
    A     "Stop"
    R     "Motor"

########## NOT
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==
Inverts the RLO. STL works with the RLO (the result of logic operation): the first check of a sequence loads it, the following ones combine it, = / S / R write it.

== Syntax ==
    NOT

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write it alone on a line.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Example ==
    A     "A"
    A     "B"
    NOT
    =     "Not_Both"

########## SET

== Syntax ==

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.

    =     "Always_On"

########## FP
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==
A rising edge of the RLO (its edge memory bit as the operand). STL works with the RLO (the result of logic operation): the first check of a sequence loads it, the following ones combine it, = / S / R write it.

== Syntax ==
    FP    <edge memory bit>

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. After the check of the signal: FP with a memory bit used for nothing else; the RLO is 1 for one cycle at the rising edge.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Timing diagram ==
  [timing] "Button"=0011110001100000 / RLO after FP=0010000001000000

== Example ==
    A     "Button"
    FP    "Edge_M"
    =     "Pulse"

########## FN
STL  ·  Bit logic

== What it does ==
A falling edge of the RLO. STL works with the RLO (the result of logic operation): the first check of a sequence loads it, the following ones combine it, = / S / R write it.

== Syntax ==
    FN    <edge memory bit>

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. After the check of the signal: FN with a memory bit used for nothing else; the RLO is 1 for one cycle at the falling edge.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Timing diagram ==
  [timing] "Button"=0011110001100000 / RLO after FN=0000001000010000

== Example ==
    A     "Button"
    FN    "Edge_M"
    =     "Pulse"

########## L
STL  ·  Load / transfer / accumulators

== What it does ==
Load the operand / a constant into ACCU 1 (ACCU 1 goes to ACCU 2). The accumulators: ACCU 1 and ACCU 2 (S7-400: ACCU 1 .. 4) are the work registers of STL; L loads ACCU 1 (the old ACCU 1 moves to ACCU 2), the math works on both, T stores ACCU 1.

== Syntax ==
    L <operand>

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Load the values with L, do the operation, transfer the result with T.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== The accumulators line by line ==
  Line | ACCU 1 | ACCU 2
  L     "A" (= 5) | 5 | ..
  L     "B" (= 3) | 3 | 5
  +I | 8 | ..
  T     "Sum" | 8 -> "Sum" | ..

== Example ==
    L     "A"
    L     "B"
    +I
    T     "Sum"

########## T
STL  ·  Load / transfer / accumulators

== What it does ==
Transfer ACCU 1 into the operand. The accumulators: ACCU 1 and ACCU 2 (S7-400: ACCU 1 .. 4) are the work registers of STL; L loads ACCU 1 (the old ACCU 1 moves to ACCU 2), the math works on both, T stores ACCU 1.

== Syntax ==
    T <operand>

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Load the values with L, do the operation, transfer the result with T.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== The accumulators line by line ==
  Line | ACCU 1 | ACCU 2
  L     "A" (= 5) | 5 | ..
  L     "B" (= 3) | 3 | 5
  +I | 8 | ..
  T     "Sum" | 8 -> "Sum" | ..

== Example ==
    L     "A"
    L     "B"
    +I
    T     "Sum"

  Line | ACCU 1 | ACCU 2

  L     "B" (= 3) | 3 | 5
  +I | 8 | ..
  T     "Sum" | 8 -> "Sum" | ..

== Example ==
the time left of T1 as BCD

########## OPN
STL  ·  Load / transfer / accumulators

== What it does ==
Open a data block: OPN DB 1, then DBW2 ... The accumulators: ACCU 1 and ACCU 2 (S7-400: ACCU 1 .. 4) are the work registers of STL; L loads ACCU 1 (the old ACCU 1 moves to ACCU 2), the math works on both, T stores ACCU 1.

== Syntax ==
    OPN <operand>

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Load the values with L, do the operation, transfer the result with T.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== The accumulators line by line ==
  Line | Meaning
  OPN   DB 1 | DB1 is the open data block
  L     DBW 2 | word 2 of DB1

== Example ==
    OPN   DB 1
    L     DBW 2
    T     "Value"

########## TAK

  L     7 | 7 | 5
  TAK | 5 | 7

== Example ==

    L     "B"
    +I
    T     "Sum"

########## CAD
STL  ·  Load / transfer / accumulators

Swap the four bytes of ACCU 1. The accumulators: ACCU 1 and ACCU 2 (S7-400: ACCU 1 .. 4) are the work registers of STL; L loads ACCU 1 (the old ACCU 1 moves to ACCU 2), the math works on both, T stores ACCU 1.

    +I
    T     "Sum"

########## SD
STL  ·  Timers (S5)

== What it does ==
S5 on delay (S_ODT). Q becomes TRUE when the start input has stayed TRUE for the time value; it goes FALSE with the start input. As TON, with the S5 timers T0 .. T255 of the S7-300/400.

== Syntax ==
    A     <start condition>
    L     S5T#2S        // the time value into ACCU 1
    SD    T 1           // start the timer with the RLO
    A     T 1           // the timer bit

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  T n | Timer | the timer: T 0 .. T 255
  ACCU 1 | S5Time | the time value: L S5T#2S before the start
  RLO | Bool | the start condition

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write the start condition (A ..), then L S5T#.. and the start instruction SD T n.
4. Read the timer later with A T n (or L T n: the time left, LC T n as BCD); R T n resets it.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Example ==
T1 as an S timer.
    A     "Start_PB"
    L     S5T#2S
    SD    T 1
    A     T 1
    =     "Motor"

== Timing diagram ==
  [timing] S=0111111000110000 / Q=0000111000000000
  (time value = 3 steps)

== Good to know ==
- The S5 timers exist on the S7-300/400 only; on an S7-1200/1500 use TON / TOF / TP.
- S5Time has a time base (10 ms .. 10 s): the larger the time, the coarser it is.
- A timer is a global resource: two places that start T1 disturb each other.

########## SE
STL  ·  Timers (S5)

== What it does ==
S5 extended pulse (S_PEXT). A rising edge starts a pulse of the time value; the pulse runs to its end even when the start input goes FALSE, a new edge starts it again.

== Syntax ==
    A     <start condition>
    L     S5T#2S        // the time value into ACCU 1
    SE    T 1           // start the timer with the RLO
    A     T 1           // the timer bit

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  T n | Timer | the timer: T 0 .. T 255
  ACCU 1 | S5Time | the time value: L S5T#2S before the start
  RLO | Bool | the start condition

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write the start condition (A ..), then L S5T#.. and the start instruction SE T n.
4. Read the timer later with A T n (or L T n: the time left, LC T n as BCD); R T n resets it.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Example ==
T1 as an S timer.
    A     "Start_PB"
    L     S5T#2S
    SE    T 1
    A     T 1
    =     "Motor"

== Timing diagram ==
  [timing] S=0100000011000000 / Q=0111000011100000
  (time value = 3 steps)

== Good to know ==
- The S5 timers exist on the S7-300/400 only; on an S7-1200/1500 use TON / TOF / TP.
- S5Time has a time base (10 ms .. 10 s): the larger the time, the coarser it is.
- A timer is a global resource: two places that start T1 disturb each other.

########## SP
STL  ·  Timers (S5)

== What it does ==
S5 pulse (S_PULSE). Q is TRUE while the start input is TRUE, at most for the time value: the pulse ends early when the input goes FALSE.

== Syntax ==
    A     <start condition>
    L     S5T#2S        // the time value into ACCU 1
    SP    T 1           // start the timer with the RLO
    A     T 1           // the timer bit

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  T n | Timer | the timer: T 0 .. T 255
  ACCU 1 | S5Time | the time value: L S5T#2S before the start
  RLO | Bool | the start condition

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write the start condition (A ..), then L S5T#.. and the start instruction SP T n.
4. Read the timer later with A T n (or L T n: the time left, LC T n as BCD); R T n resets it.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Example ==
T1 as an S timer.
    A     "Start_PB"
    L     S5T#2S
    SP    T 1
    A     T 1
    =     "Motor"

== Timing diagram ==
  [timing] S=0111111001000000 / Q=0111000001000000
  (time value = 3 steps: the second pulse ends with the input)

== Good to know ==
- The S5 timers exist on the S7-300/400 only; on an S7-1200/1500 use TON / TOF / TP.
- S5Time has a time base (10 ms .. 10 s): the larger the time, the coarser it is.
- A timer is a global resource: two places that start T1 disturb each other.

########## SS
STL  ·  Timers (S5)

== What it does ==
S5 retentive on delay (S_ODTS). A rising edge starts the time, which runs to its end even when the input goes FALSE; then Q stays TRUE until the timer is reset (R).

== Syntax ==
    A     <start condition>
    L     S5T#2S        // the time value into ACCU 1
    SS    T 1           // start the timer with the RLO
    A     T 1           // the timer bit

== Parameters ==
  Name | Data type | Meaning
  T n | Timer | the timer: T 0 .. T 255
  ACCU 1 | S5Time | the time value: L S5T#2S before the start
  RLO | Bool | the start condition

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write the start condition (A ..), then L S5T#.. and the start instruction SS T n.
4. Read the timer later with A T n (or L T n: the time left, LC T n as BCD); R T n resets it.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Example ==
T1 as an S timer.
    A     "Start_PB"
    L     S5T#2S
    SS    T 1
    A     T 1
    =     "Motor"

== Timing diagram ==
  [timing] S=0100000000000000 / R=0000000000110000 / Q=0000111111000000
  (time value = 3 steps: Q stays until R)

== Good to know ==
- The S5 timers exist on the S7-300/400 only; on an S7-1200/1500 use TON / TOF / TP.
- S5Time has a time base (10 ms .. 10 s): the larger the time, the coarser it is.
- A timer is a global resource: two places that start T1 disturb each other.

########## SF
STL  ·  Timers (S5)

== What it does ==
S5 off delay (S_OFFDT). Q follows the input to TRUE; when the input goes FALSE, Q stays TRUE for the time value.

4. Read the timer later with A T n (or L T n: the time left, LC T n as BCD); R T n resets it.

== Example ==
T1 as an S timer.
    A     "Start_PB"
    L     S5T#2S
    SF    T 1
    A     T 1
    =     "Motor"

== Timing diagram ==
  [timing] S=0111100000000000 / Q=0111111100000000
  (time value = 3 steps)

== Good to know ==
- The S5 timers exist on the S7-300/400 only; on an S7-1200/1500 use TON / TOF / TP.
- S5Time has a time base (10 ms .. 10 s): the larger the time, the coarser it is.
- A timer is a global resource: two places that start T1 disturb each other.

########## CU
STL  ·  Counters (S5)

== What it does ==
S5 counter count up: a rising edge of the RLO adds 1 to the counter C n (0 .. 999). The counter bit is TRUE while the value is not 0.

== Syntax ==
    A     <condition>
    CU    C 1

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write the condition (A ..), then CU C n.
4. L C n loads the value, LC C n as BCD, A C n reads the bit.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Example ==
counts up, "Load" sets 10.
    A     "Box_Sensor"
    CU    C 1
    A     "Load"
    L     C#10
    S     C 1
    L     C 1
    T     "Count"

== Good to know ==
- The S5 counters exist on the S7-300/400 only; on an S7-1200/1500 use CTU / CTD / CTUD.
- The value is 0 .. 999.

########## CD
STL  ·  Counters (S5)

== What it does ==
S5 counter count down: a rising edge subtracts 1 from C n (not below 0).

== Syntax ==
    A     <condition>
    CD    C 1

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write the condition (A ..), then CD C n.
4. L C n loads the value, LC C n as BCD, A C n reads the bit.
5. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Example ==
counts up, "Load" sets 10.
    A     "Box_Sensor"
    CU    C 1
    A     "Load"
    L     C#10
    S     C 1
    L     C 1
    T     "Count"

== Good to know ==
- The S5 counters exist on the S7-300/400 only; on an S7-1200/1500 use CTU / CTD / CTUD.
- The value is 0 .. 999.

########## +I
STL  ·  Integer math

== What it does ==
ACCU 2 + ACCU 1 (Int); also -I *I /I. The result is in ACCU 1; the status bits (OV, OS, CC0/1) tell an overflow or the sign.

== Syntax ==
    +I

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. L the first value, L the second one (both Int), then the instruction, then T the result.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== The accumulators line by line ==
  Line | ACCU 1 | ACCU 2
  L     "A" (= 5) | 5 | ..
  L     "B" (= 3) | 3 | 5
  +I | 8 | ..

== Example ==
    L     "A"
    L     "B"
    +I
    T     "Result"

== Good to know ==
- The types are not checked: +I adds the lower words as Int, +R the double words as Real. Load the right type.

########## +
STL  ·  Integer math

== What it does ==

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.

  Line | ACCU 1
  L     "Count" (= 5) | 5
  + 1 | 6
  T     "Count" | -> "Count"

== Example ==
    L     "Count"
    + 1
    T     "Count"

== Good to know ==
- The types are not checked: +I adds the lower words as Int, +R the double words as Real. Load the right type.

########## ==I
STL  ·  Comparison

== What it does ==
RLO := ACCU 2 = ACCU 1 (Int); also <>I >I <I >=I <=I, D for DInt, R for Real. The result of the comparison is the RLO: it can be combined (A ..) and written (=).

== Syntax ==
    L     <value 1>
    L     <value 2>
    ==I
    =     <bit>

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. L both values, then the comparison; the RLO is TRUE when ACCU 2 == ACCU 1.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== The same in LAD ==
  [LAD] cmp@Int=== "Level"|80;coil "High"

== Example ==
    L     "Level"
    L     80
    ==I
    =     "High"

########## ITD
STL  ·  Conversion

== What it does ==
Int -> DInt. The conversion works on ACCU 1.

== Syntax ==
    L     <value>
    ITD
    T     <target>

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. L the value, the conversion, T the result into an operand of the new type.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Values ==
  ACCU 1 before (Int) | after (DInt)
  -5 | -5
  32767 | 32767

== Example ==
Int -> DInt -> Real (an Int to Real takes two steps).
    L     "Count_I"
    ITD
    DTR
    T     "Count_R"

########## DTR

  before (Real) | RND | RND+ | RND- | TRUNC
  2.5 | 2 | 3 | 2 | 2
  2.6 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 2
  -2.6 | -3 | -2 | -3 | -2

== Example ==

    DTR
    T     "Count_R"

    T     <target>

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. L the value, the conversion, T the result into an operand of the new type.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Values ==
  before (Int) | after (BCD)
  123 | W#16#0123
  -45 | W#16#F045

== Example ==
Int -> DInt -> Real (an Int to Real takes two steps).
    L     "Count_I"
    ITD
    DTR
    T     "Count_R"

########## INVI
STL  ·  Conversion

== What it does ==
The ones complement of the Int; INVD for DInt. The conversion works on ACCU 1.

== Syntax ==
    L     <value>
    INVI
    T     <target>

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. L the value, the conversion, T the result into an operand of the new type.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Values ==
  before | after
  W#16#00FF | W#16#FF00

== Example ==
Int -> DInt -> Real (an Int to Real takes two steps).
    L     "Count_I"
    ITD
    DTR
    T     "Count_R"

########## AW
STL  ·  Word logic / shift / rotate

== What it does ==
ACCU 1 AND ACCU 2 (word) or AND a constant: AW W#16#00FF; also OW XOW, AD OD XOD.

== Syntax ==
    AW <value>

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. L the word, then AW with a constant (or L a second word and AW without one), T the result.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== The bits ==
  [bits] ACCU 1 = 16#5AF3=0101101011110011 / AW W#16#00FF = 16#00F3=00000000iiiiooii

== Example ==
    L     "Status"
    AW    W#16#00FF
    T     "Low_Byte"

########## SLW
STL  ·  Word logic / shift / rotate

== What it does ==
Shift the low word left by n (or by ACCU 2); SRW SLD SRD, SSI SSD with the sign.

== Syntax ==
    SLW <value>

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. L the value, SLW n (the number of places), T the result.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== The bits ==
  [bits] ACCU 1 = 16#00B3=0000000010110011 / SLW 3 = 16#0598=0000010110011ooo

== Example ==
    L     "Pattern"
    SLW   3
    T     "Pattern"

########## JU
STL  ·  Jumps

== What it does ==
Jump to a label (M001: ..). A jump goes to a label in the same network: the lines between are skipped.

== Syntax ==
    JU    <label>
    ..
    <label>: ..

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write the jump with a label (up to 4 characters), and the same label with a colon before the line where it goes.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Flowchart ==
  [flowchart] jump|JU|the lines skipped|M001: the lines after the label

== Example ==
          JU    M001
          L     1        // skipped
          T     "X"
    M001: NOP 0

########## JC
STL  ·  Jumps

== What it does ==
Jump when RLO = 1; JCN when 0. A jump goes to a label in the same network: the lines between are skipped.

== Syntax ==
    JC    <label>
    ..
    <label>: ..

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write the jump with a label (up to 4 characters), and the same label with a colon before the line where it goes.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Flowchart ==
  [flowchart] jump|JC|the lines skipped|M001: the lines after the label

== Example ==
In manual the automatic speed is not written.
          A     "Manual"
          JC    M001
          L     "Speed_Auto"
          T     "Speed"
    M001: NOP 0

########## LOOP
STL  ·  Jumps

== What it does ==
Count the low word of ACCU 1 down, jump while it is not 0. A jump goes to a label in the same network: the lines between are skipped.

== Syntax ==
    LOOP    <label>
    ..
    <label>: ..

== How to use it ==
1. Click into an STL network at the line where the instruction goes (the caret). One instruction per line.
2. Double click the element in this list, drag it by its grip into the code, or right button: Insert at the caret.
3. Write the jump with a label (up to 4 characters), and the same label with a colon before the line where it goes.
4. Compile (F7), download (F8) and RUN (F5). Monitor (F9) shows the value of every line beside the code.

== Flowchart ==
  [flowchart] loop|L 10 / T #n|#n <> 0|the lines of the loop|LOOP: #n - 1

== Example ==
Runs the lines 10 times.
          L     10
    NEXT: T     #n
          // the lines of the loop
          L     #n
          LOOP  NEXT

      In := 
      Out := "Start_PB"
